Generalised Legendrian racks of Legendrian links
Biswadeep Karmakar, Deepanshi Saraf, Mahender Singh

Abstract
. A generalised Legendrian rack is a rack equipped with a Legendrian structure, which is a pair of maps encoding the information of Legendrian Reidemeister moves together with up and down cusps in the front diagram of an oriented Legendrian link. Employing a purely rack theoretic approach, we associate a generalised Legendrian rack (or a G ​ L \operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004} -rack) to an oriented Legendrian link, and prove that it is an invariant under Legendrian isotopy. As immediate applications, we prove that this invariant distinguishes infinitely many oriented Legendrian unknots and oriented Legendrian trefoils. To comprehend their algebraic structure, we prove that every G ​ L \operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004} -rack admits a homogeneous representation. Further, using the idea of trunks, we define modules over G ​ L \operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004} -racks, and prove the equivalence of the category of G ​ L \operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004} -rack modules and the category of Beck modules over a fixed G ​ L \operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004} -rack.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 57K10, 57K12; Secondary 57K33

1.
Introduction

Racks are algebraic structures equipped with distributive binary operations. These objects are used for constructing invariants of framed topological links in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
, and they also give non-degenerate solutions of the Yang-Baxter equation. Quandles are stronger avatars of racks, which encode algebraically the three Reidemeister moves of planar diagrams of oriented topological links in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
. The study of racks and quandles gained thrust after the pioneering works of Joyce
[
18
]
and Matveev
[
22
]
, who showed that fundamental quandles are complete invariants of non-split links up to orientation of the ambient space. Although fundamental quandles are strong invariants of oriented topological links, the isomorphism problem for them is difficult. This motivated search for invariants of quandles themselves, most notably, (co)homological invariants.

A (co)homology theory for racks was introduced in
[
14
,
15
]
as homology of the associated rack space of a rack. This led to the development of a (co)homology theory for quandles in
[
4
]
, where state-sum invariants using quandle cocycles as weights were defined for oriented topological links. Giving a new perspective to quandle cohomology in terms of model categories, the recent work
[
23
]
showed that quandle cohomology is a Quillen cohomology.

There are various generalisations and refinements of classical knot theory. In this paper, we consider a geometrically motivated refinement, namely, the Legendrian knot theory. We equip
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
with the standard contact structure, which is the kernel of the contact form
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29050\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29049\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29048
. Then, a Legendrian link is a smooth link in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
such that it is tangent to the standard contact structure at each of its points. Analogous to topological links, Legendrian links are studied upto Legendrian isotopy and via appropriate planar projections called front projections. Each Legendrian link admits a front projection with finitely many crossings and cusps. An analogue of the classical Reidemeister Theorem is also known in the Legendrian setting, which makes the diagram based study of these objects feasible. Many invariants of Legendrian links are already known in the literature. See, for example
[
6
,
7
]
, and the survey article
[
13
]
for more details. It turns out that Legendrian isotopic Legendrian links are smoothly isotopic, but smoothly isotopic Legendrian links may not be Legendrian isotopic. For instance, there are infinitely many Legendrian knots (up to Legendrian isotopy) that are smoothly isotopic to the unknot. In fact, a complete classification of Legendrian unknots is already known
[
10
,
11
]
. Thus, the classification problem of Legendrian links may be considered as a refinement of the classification problem of topological links.

It is intriguing to know whether there are analogues of racks and related (co)homology theories for studying Legendrian links. Furthermore, it is natural to ask whether such an invariant for Legendrian links recovers the corresponding known invariant for underlying topological links. We attempt to address these questions by introducing generalised Legendrian racks (or simply
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks), which, informally speaking, are racks together with a pair of maps that encode the information of Legendrian Reidemeister moves along with the information of upward and downward cusps of an oriented front diagram. Our construction strengthens and enhances the preceding rack-theoretic invariants appearing in
[
5
]
and
[
21
]
. Note that, the rack invariant considered in
[
5
]
already recovers the one appearing in
[
21
]
, and that
[
5
]
is focussed on colouring invariants obtained from finite Legendrian racks. Our notion of a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack of an oriented Legendrian link also appears to be an appropriate refinement of the fundamental quandle of the underlying oriented topological link. We shall see that bi-Legendrian rack introduced in
[
20
]
is equivalent to the
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack that we introduce here. Further, the focus of
[
20
]
is again coloring invariants of Legendrian knots. As an application of our construction, we are able to immediately distinguish infinitely many oriented Legendrian unknots and trefoils. We then proceed to explore the algebraic and categorical properties of
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks. Further, we also identify the appropriate coefficient objects for defining (co)homology theories for
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks. Our approach is purely rack theoretic, and does not incorporate any geometric ideas from the theory of Legendrian links.

The paper is organised as follows. In Section
2
, we recall some basics from Legendrian knot theory. In Section
3
, we define a generalised Legendrian rack, and give many examples of these structures. In Section
4
, we define the generalised Legendrian rack of an oriented Legendrian link, and prove that it is invariant under the Legendrian equivalence (Theorem
4.3
). As an application, we deduce that this invariant distinguishes infinitely many oriented Legendrian unknots (Theorem
4.6
), and infinitely many oriented Legendrian trefoils (Theorem
4.7
). Furthermore, it is proved that two topologically distinct non-split oriented Legendrian links cannot have isomorphic generalised Legendrian racks (Theorem
4.11
). We also discuss the idea of colouring of an oriented Legendrian link by a generalised Legendrian rack, and deduce that it gives an invariant of Legendrian links (Corollary
4.13
). Adapting the idea of Joyce to the Legendrian setting, in Section
5
, we prove that every generalised Legendrian rack admits a homogeneous representation as a generalised Legendrian rack which is built up with a disjoint union of cosets of a family of subgroups of some appropriate group (Theorem
5.2
). In Section
6
, we define appropriate modules over
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks, which could be used for introducing (co)homology theories for
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks, and give some examples. In Theorem
7.4
of Section
7
, we prove the equivalence of the category of
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack modules and the category of Beck modules over a fixed
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack.

2.
Contact structure and Legendrian links

We begin by recalling some basic ideas from contact topology that are essential for our discussions. The reader may refer to
[
9
,
12
,
16
]
for finer details. We restrict ourselves to 3-manifolds, specifically
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
, and refer the reader to the excellent survey article
[
13
]
on Legendrian knots.

Definition 2.1
.

A 1-form
\mathchar 28939
on an oriented 3-dimensional manifold
\mathchar 29005
is called a
contact form
if
\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29048}\mathchar 8798\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29048}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720
for all
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005
. A hyperplane distribution
\mathchar 28952
is a
contact structure
on
\mathchar 29005
if it is the kernel of a contact form, that is,
\mathchar 28952\mathchar 12349\ker\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28939\delimiter 84054785
for a contact 1-form
\mathchar 28939
on
\mathchar 29005
. A manifold
\mathchar 29005
equipped with a contact structure
\mathchar 28952
is called a
contact manifold
, and denoted as
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29005\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28952\delimiter 84054785
.

Here,
\mathchar 28952
being a hyperplane distribution means that
\mathchar 28952_{\mathchar 29048}
is a two dimensional subspace of the tangent space
\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29048}\mathchar 29005
at each
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29005
. We also require that
\mathchar 28939\mathchar 8798\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28939
defines the given orientation on
\mathchar 29005
. The contact condition
\mathchar 28939\mathchar 8798\mathchar 29028\mathchar 28939\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720
is a
total non-integrability
condition, which ensures that there is no embedded surface in
\mathchar 29005
which is tangent to
\mathchar 28952
everywhere.

Example 2.2
.

The simplest contact structure on
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
(with respect to the Cartesian coordinates
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84054785
) is given by

\mathchar 28952^{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\textrm{span}~\left\{{{\mathchar 320\over\mathchar 320\mathchar 29049}}\mathchar 24891~{{\mathchar 320\over\mathchar 320\mathchar 29048}}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29049{{\mathchar 320\over\mathchar 320\mathchar 29050}}\right\}\mathchar 24891

which is the kernel of the contact form

\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29050\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29049\mathchar 29028\mathchar 29048\mathchar 314

The contact structure
\mathchar 28952^{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29028}
is called the
standard contact structure
on
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
. Note that the hyperplanes along the
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 29050
-plane are all horizontal, that is, parallel to the
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 29049
-plane. Moving along the positive
\mathchar 29049
-axis from the origin, the hyperplanes start to twist around the
\mathchar 29049
-axis in a left handed manner with the planes making a
\mathchar 28729\mathchar 28720^{\mathchar 8718}
twist at infinity (see Figure
1
, taken from
[
13
, Section 2.1]
).

Figure 1.
Standard contact structure on
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
.

There are many contact structures on a 3-manifold
\mathchar 29005
. But, by Darboux’s Theorem, any point in
\mathchar 29005
has an open neighborhood that is diffeomorphic to an open neighborhood of the origin in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
by a diffeomorphism that takes the contact structure on
\mathchar 29005
to the standard contact structure
\mathchar 28952^{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29028}
on
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
. Throughout the article, the contact form
\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28720}
and the contact structure
\mathchar 28952^{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29028}
on
\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
are fixed.

Definition 2.3
.

A
Legendrian knot
in
\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28952^{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
is a smooth embedding
\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12346\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12833\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}
such that
\mathchar 28941^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28952^{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28941\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785}\text{ for all }\mathchar 28946\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 314

As in topological knot theory, planar projections play a crucial role in Legendrian knot theory. Let
\mathchar 28941\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
be a parametrized Legendrian knot in
\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28952^{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
. Then the
front projection
of
\mathchar 28941
is the map
\mathchar 28953\mathchar 12346\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12833\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28722}
given by
\mathchar 28953\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
. A parametrization of a Legendrian knot will induce an orientation on the knot, and hence we consider oriented Legendrian knots by choosing this parametrization induced orientation.

Note that a parametrized Legendrian knot
\mathchar 28941
satisfies the equation
\mathchar 29050^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29049\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29048^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785
. There is no point on the front projection where the tangent is vertical, instead we have cusps on both right and left side. At cusp points, we have
\mathchar 29048^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29050^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
. Moving away from cusps, the
\mathchar 29049
-coordinate can be recovered as
\mathchar 29049\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 29050^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785\over\mathchar 29048^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946\delimiter 84054785}}
. We note that, at a crossing in the front projection, the strand with the lower slope will always be above. For example, see Figure
2
for the front projection of a Legendrian trefoil. A
Legendrian link
can be defined analogously by replacing
\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28721}
with the disjoint union
\mathchar 8820_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29038}~\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28721}
.

Figure 2.
A Legendrian trefoil.

Definition 2.4
.

A
front diagram
is an immersion of a disjoint union of circles in the
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 29050
-plane such that the following hold:

(1)

The immersion is an embedding at all but finitely many points. At these points, there is a cusp or a double point.

(2)

There is no vertical tangency at any point.

The planar Legendrian isotopy between two front diagrams is defined in the usual way. We note that every Legendrian link gives rise to a front diagram by taking the front projection. Conversely, given a front diagram, there is a unique Legendrian link whose front projection agrees with this front diagram.

Definition 2.5
.

Two Legendrian links
\mathchar 29003
and
\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 560}
are called
Legendrian equivalent
if there exists a smooth map
\mathchar 28998\mathchar 12346\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12833\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28952^{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
such that the following hold:

(1)

\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\{\mathchar 28720\}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29003
.

(2)

\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\{\mathchar 28721\}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 560}
.

(3)

\mathchar 28998\delimiter 69640972_{\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\{\mathchar 29044\}}
is a Legendrian embedding for each
\mathchar 29044\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84267779
.

The local moves of a front diagram shown in Figure
3
are called
Legendrian Reidemeister moves
, written LR moves for short.

Figure 3.
Legendrian Reidemeister moves.

An analogue of the Reidemeister Theorem for topological links is known for Legendrian links
[
8
,
24
]
.

Theorem 2.6
.

Two Legendrian links
\mathchar 29003
and
\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 560}
are Legendrian equivalent if and only if any front diagram
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
of
\mathchar 29003
can be obtained from any front diagram
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
of
\mathchar 29003^{\mathchar 560}
by a finite sequence of Legendrian Reidemeister moves and planar Legendrian isotopy.

In the pursuit of answering the formidable problem of classifying Legendrian links, several invariants have been studied in the literature. We briefly recall a few classical invariants here, and refer the reader to
[
13
]
for a detailed and geometric explanation of the same.

The simplest invariant of a Legendrian link is its
topological link type
. The next invariant, called the
Thurston-Bennequin invariant
and denoted by
\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29026\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
, can be computed from the oriented front diagram
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
of
\mathchar 29003
as

\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29026\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathrm{\mathchar 29047\mathchar 29042\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29032\mathchar 29029}\delimiter 67273472\mathit{\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8704{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 67273472\mathrm{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29037\mathchar 29026\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29042~\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29030~\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29043~\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29038~}\mathit{\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Another well-known invariant is the
rotation number

\mathchar 29042\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
of the Legendrian link
\mathchar 29003
, which is computed from the oriented front diagram
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
of
\mathchar 29003
as

\mathchar 29042\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28996\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29013\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891

where
\mathchar 28996
is the number of down cusps and
\mathchar 29013
is the number of up cusps in
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
.

It turns out that these classical invariants, although powerful, are not enough to distinguish several Legendrian links, which led to development of more sophisticated invariants such as Chekanov-Eliashberg DGA
[
6
]
, etc.

3.
generalised Legendrian racks

Recall that, a
rack

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\delimiter 84054785
is a set
\mathchar 29016
with a binary operation
\mathchar 8707
satisfying the following:

(1)

Given
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29016
, there exists a unique
\mathchar 29050\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29016
such that
\mathchar 29050\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
.

(2)

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29050\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84054785}
for all
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29016
.

A
quandle
is a rack with the additional axiom of idempotency, that is,
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
for all
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29016
. For example, each group
\mathchar 28999
turns into a quandle
\operatorname{\mathchar 28995\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\delimiter 84054785
with respect to the binary operation
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29049^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29048\mathchar 29049
of conjugation. A generalised Legendrian rack is a rack with an additional structure and its defining axioms are derived from the oriented Legendrian Reidemeister moves.

Definition 3.1
.

A
generalised Legendrian rack
or a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack is a quadruple
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
, where
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\delimiter 84054785
is a rack and
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29016
are maps such that the
following hold for all
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29016

(L1)

\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
.

(L1
′
)

\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
.

(L2)

\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049
.

(L2
′
)

\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049
.

(L3)

\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049
.

(L3
′
)

\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049

We refer the pair
\delimiter 67273472\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
as a
generalised Legendrian structure or a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-structure
on the rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\delimiter 84054785

Note that, if
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
is a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\delimiter 84054785

\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 12349\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}
. We begin with the following observations.

Proposition 3.2
.

Let

(1)

The maps

and
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}
are automorphisms of the underlying rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\delimiter 84054785
.

(2)

\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
and
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
for all
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29016
.

(3)

\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785
for all
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29016
.

(4)

If the underlying rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\delimiter 84054785
is involutory,
\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 314\mathchar 29029\mathchar 314}
,
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29049
for all
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29016
, then
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
is also a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack.

Proof.

Axioms (L2) and (L3) imply that
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}
is an endomorphism of
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\delimiter 84054785
. Axiom (L1) immediately gives surjectivity of
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}
. Finally, axiom (L1
′
) together with
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}
being a homomorphism implies that
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}
is injective as well. A similar argument shows that
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}
is also an automorphism of
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\delimiter 84054785
, which proves assertion (1).

Axiom (L1) together with the fact that
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}
and
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}
are homomorphisms gives
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
. Now, axioms (L3) and (L3
′
) gives
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
. Similarly, one obtains
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
, proving assertion (2), which further yields assertion (3) immdediately.

Applying
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
to
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049
and
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049
shows that
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
satisfies (L3) and (L2), respectively. Similarly,
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
satisfies (L3
′
) and (L2
′
).
Assertion (2) gives
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
, which implies that
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785
. Since
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\delimiter 84054785
is involutory, this gives
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785
. Further, since

\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}
and
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}
are homomorphisms, we obtain
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785
. A similar argument yields

∎

-structure on

for more examples of finite type with

. Let us construct another family of examples. Recall that a

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707_{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 84054785
is the rack structure on a set
\mathchar 29016

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707_{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 84054785

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
\mathchar 29016
be a set and
\mathchar 28955
a permutation of
\mathchar 29016
. Then, a pair
\delimiter 67273472\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
of maps is a

-structure on the permutation rack

if and only if
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28955^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 314

Proof.

Corollary 3.6
.

Let
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707_{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 84054785
be a permutation rack. Then, the pairs
\delimiter 67273472\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28955^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
and
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28955^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}\delimiter 84054785
are
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-structures on
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707_{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 84054785
. Further, if
\mathchar 28955^{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 12349\mathrm{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}
, then the pair
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28955\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28955\delimiter 84054785
is also a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-structures on
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707_{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 84054785
.

The preceding corollary provides us a large supply of non-trivial
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks. It is a natural question to determine all possible
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-structures on a given rack. In the case when
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}
, we have the following observation for finite permutation racks.

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
\mathchar 29016
be a finite set with more than one element and
\mathchar 28955
a permutation of
\mathchar 29016
. Suppose that there exists an even integer
\mathchar 29035
such that
\mathchar 28955
is a product of odd number of
\mathchar 29035
-cycles. Then the permutation rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707_{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 84054785
does not admit any
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-structure of the form
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}
.

Proof.

Note that a permutation
\mathchar 28954
of
\mathchar 29016
is a perfect square in the symmetric group on
\mathchar 29016
if and only if, for every even integer
\mathchar 29035
, the cycle decomposition of
\mathchar 28954
has an even number of
\mathchar 29035
-cycles. Hence, the given permutation
\mathchar 28955
cannot be a perfect square. Now, if
\delimiter 67273472\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
is a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-structure on the permutation rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707_{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 84054785
with
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}
, then Proposition
3.5
gives
\mathchar 28955\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}
, a contradiction. Hence,
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707_{\mathchar 28955}\delimiter 84054785
does not admit any
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-structure of the form
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}
.
∎

Remark 3.8
.

We note that there is a curious similarity between defining axioms of Legendrian racks considered in
[
5
, Definition 4]
and quandles with good involutions considered in
[
19
, Definition 2.1]
. The latter objects have been introduced for coloring unoriented links, and a cohomology theory has also been developed for the same.

We conclude this section by recording some basic categorical aspects of this theory. Let
\mathbf{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
denote the category of
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks. Its objects are the
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks and the morphisms are as defined above. It is clear that finite direct products and equalisers exist in
\mathbf{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
, where the latter are simply the set-theoretic equalisers with the induced
\mathchar 8707
,
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29016}
and
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}
. Since
\mathbf{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
has all binary products and equalisers, they admit all finite limits
[
2
, Proposition 2.8.2]
.

Let
\mathbf{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29016}
denote the slice category over a fixed
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}\delimiter 84054785
. The objects in
\mathbf{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29016}
are the
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack homomorphisms
\mathchar 29030\mathchar 12346\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8511\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29017}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}\delimiter 84054785
, and a morphism from
\mathchar 29030\mathchar 12346\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8511\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29017}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}\delimiter 84054785
to
\mathchar 29031\mathchar 12346\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29018\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8494\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29018}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}\delimiter 84054785
is a

-rack homomorphism
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12346\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8511\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29017}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29017}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29018\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8494\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29018}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29018}\delimiter 84054785

. Since

4.

Notation.

Let us set some notational conventions for the rest of the paper.

(1)

Let
\mathchar 29016
be a non-empty set and
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\delimiter 84054785
the universe of words generated by
\mathchar 29016
as described above. If
\mathchar 29010
is a given set of relations among elements of
\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\delimiter 84054785
, then
\mathopen{\hbox{${\delimiter 69632778}$}\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\delimiter 69632778}$}}\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29010\mathclose{\hbox{${\delimiter 86414091}$}\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\delimiter 86414091}$}}
denotes the
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack whose elements are equivalence classes of elements of
\mathchar 28998\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\delimiter 84054785
modulo the equivalence relation generated by
\mathchar 29010
.

(2)

We write elements of
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks as left-associated products. Indeed, it follows from
[
25
, Lemma 4.4.7]
that any element in a rack can be written in a left-associated form

\left\delimiter 67273472\left\delimiter 67273472\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\left\delimiter 67273472\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28721}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28722}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 28723}}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 24891

which, for simplicity, we write as

\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 314

We now associate a natural
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack to an oriented Legendrian link. Let
\mathchar 29003
be an oriented Legendrian link and
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
its front diagram with the induced orientation. Let
\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 28722}\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29048_{\mathchar 29038}\}
be a set with one element for each strand of
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
, where a strand means a connected segment of an arc in the front diagram
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
, which begins and ends at a crossing or a cusp. In other words, a strand does not contain any cusp. At each crossing, we take the usual relation as in the fundamental quandle of the underlying oriented topological link (see Figure
4
(i) and
4
(ii)), and refer to such a relation as a
crossing relation
. The difference arises at cusps. More precisely, if the strand
\mathchar 29048
connects to the strand
\mathchar 29049
at a cusp by moving along the orientation in the positive
\mathchar 29050
-direction (see Figure
4
(iii)), then we impose the relation

\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

Similarly, if the strand
\mathchar 29048
connects to the strand
\mathchar 29049
at a cusp by moving along the orientation in the negative
\mathchar 29050
-direction (see Figure
4
(iv)), then we impose the relation

\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

We refer to such relations as
cusp relations
. Then, the
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
associated to the front diagram
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
is defined to be the set of equivalence classes of elements of
\mathchar 28998\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\delimiter 84054785
modulo the equivalence relation generated by the crossing and the cusp relations.

Theorem 4.3
.

The generalised Legendrian rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
of a front diagram
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
of an oriented Legendrian link
\mathchar 29003
is invariant under the Legendrian Reidemeister moves. More precisely, the generalised Legendrian rack is an invariant of oriented Legendrian links.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three parts, one for each type of oriented Legendrian Reidemeister move.

LR1 moves.
Consider two front diagrams of
\mathchar 29003
which differ only by the first LR1 move. The LHS of Figure
5
contributes four generators
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29047
and three relations
\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29047
,
\mathchar 29050\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 29047\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29028\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84054785
to presentation of the
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack of
\mathchar 29003
. An elementary check shows that these relations are equivalent to the relations
\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048
,
\mathchar 29050\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 29047\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
. Thus, the generators
\mathchar 29049\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29047
from the LHS can be expressed in terms of the generator
\mathchar 29048
and the Legendrian structure
\delimiter 67273472\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 84054785
. At the same time,
\mathchar 29048
is the only generator on the RHS of Figure
5
. Hence, the resulting
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks of both the front diagrams are isomorphic.

\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}

\mathchar 29003

\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049

\mathchar 29026\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 29027\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29049

Second LR2 move.

The other oriented LR2 moves apart from these can be dealt with similarly.

LR3 moves.
The proof of invariance of the Legendrian rack under LR3 moves is the same as that of the fundamental quandle of an oriented topological link. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

In view of Theorem
4.3
, we have the following definition.

Definition 4.4
.

The generalised Legendrian rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
of an oriented Legendrian link
\mathchar 29003
is defined to be
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
for some front diagram
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
of
\mathchar 29003
.

Remark 4.5
.

We note that the
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
of an oriented Legendrian link
\mathchar 29003
is finitely generated as a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack since each front diagram has only finitely many crossings and cusps. At the same time, it must be noted that the maps
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}
and
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}
are not explicit in general.

The following result shows that
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks distinguish infinitely many oriented Legendrian unknots.

The Legendrian unknots
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785

Theorem 4.7
.

The Legendrian trefoils
\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\delimiter 84054785

) are not Legendrian isotopic for
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\delimiter 84054785

\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack, one can conclude that the presentation of the Legendrian trefoil
\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785

Similarly, we have

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathopen{\hbox{${\delimiter 69632778}$}\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\delimiter 69632778}$}}\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}~\mathchar 12906~\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 352}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785\mathclose{\hbox{${\delimiter 86414091}$}\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\delimiter 86414091}$}}\mathchar 314

Since
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\delimiter 84054785

. Consider the

\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
. Then
\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
and
\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 352}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28955^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
. Similarly,
\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
and
\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28955\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 8707^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28955\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
. Thus,
\mathchar 28958
is a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack homomorphism. For the other trefoil, suppose that there exists a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack homomorphism
\mathchar 28960\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28723}
defined by
\mathchar 28960\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
and
\mathchar 28960\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29049
for some
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28723}
. Applying
\mathchar 28960
to the relations gives
\mathchar 28955^{\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
, which is not possible as
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29035
. Hence,
\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\delimiter 84054785
are not Legendrian isotopic for
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\delimiter 84054785
.

Remark 4.8
.

Theorems
4.6
and
4.7
provide us a good deal of examples with the following properties.

(1)

Rotation number does not differentiate the following pairs of knots but
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack does:

(a)

Unknots of the type
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785
for
\mathchar 29037\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29038
.

(b)

Trefoils of the type
\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 352\mathchar 24891\mathchar 352\delimiter 84054785
for
\mathchar 29035\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 352
.

(2)

Thurston-Bennequin number does not differentiate the following pairs of knots but
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack does:

(a)

Unknots of the type
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 29013\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037\delimiter 84054785
for odd
\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
.

(b)

Trefoils of the type
\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 29012\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785
for odd
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
.

Remark 4.9
.

have isomorphic
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}

[

, Theorem 1.5]
that these two unknots are Legendrian isotopic.

Theorem 4.11
.

If
\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28721}
and
\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28722}
are two topologically distinct non-split oriented Legendrian links, then
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{\mathchar 566{{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}}}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
.

Proof.

Note that the fundamental quandle of the underlying oriented topological link can be obtained from the
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack of the oriented Legendrian link by adding the relation
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29048
for each generators
\mathchar 29048
of the
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack. Further, recall that the fundamental quandle is a complete invariant of a non-split oriented link upto orientation and mirror-isotopy. Since
\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28721}
and
\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28722}
are topologically distinct non-split oriented Legendrian links, it follows that
\mathchar 29009\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{\mathchar 566{{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}}}}\mathchar 29009\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785
, and hence
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{\mathchar 566{{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\textstyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}}}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
.
∎

Motivated by theorems
4.6
and
4.7
, we define the notion of colouring of an oriented Legendrian link by a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack.

Definition 4.12
.

Let
\mathchar 29003
be an oriented Legendrian link,
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
its front diagram and
\mathchar 29011
the set of strands in
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
. A coloring of
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
by a given
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8511\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
is a map
\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29011\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29016
such that

(1)

\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8511\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84054785
,

(2)

\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
,

(3)

\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
,

where
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29050\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29025\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29026
are the strands as shown in Figure
12
.

Coloring conditions at crossings and cusps.

It is not difficult to see that the total number of colorings of
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
by
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8511\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
is an invariant of the link
\mathchar 29004
, that is, it does not depend on the front diagram of
\mathchar 29003
. Furthermore, it follows from the construction of
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
that the number of colorings equals the cardinality of the set
\operatorname{\mathchar 29000\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037}_{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29016\delimiter 84054785
of all
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack homomorphisms from
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
to
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8511\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
. In view of Theorem
4.3
, we have the following result.

Corollary 4.13
.

If
\mathchar 29003
is an oriented Legendrian link and
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8511\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack, then
\operatorname{\mathchar 29000\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037}_{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29016\delimiter 84054785
is an invariant of
\mathchar 29003
.

Remark 4.14
.

It follows from the definition of a morphism between two
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks and Proposition
4.1
that a morphism from
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
to
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8511\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}^{\mathchar 560}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}^{\mathchar 560}\delimiter 84054785
is completely determined by its values on the set of strands in a front diagram
\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\delimiter 84054785
of
\mathchar 29003
. Since the set of strands is always finite, the set
\operatorname{\mathchar 29000\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037}_{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29003\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29016\delimiter 84054785
is also finite whenever
\mathchar 29016
is finite.

.

Every

For any
\mathchar 28952\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}
, we have

\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28952\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28952\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 28952\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 24891

and hence
\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28952\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}
. Similarly, one can show that
\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28952\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28950\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}
. It follows from axiom (L1) that

\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891

and hence
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}
. Similarly, it follows that
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 28950\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}
. Also, axiom (L3) implies that

\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785

and
\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 28950\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785
for all
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29016
. Hence, Proposition
5.1
yields the Legendrian rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8820_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}~\mathchar 28999\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891~\{\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 24891~\{\mathchar 29042_{\mathchar 29033}\}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 24891~\{\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29033}\}_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785
. It can be seen that the orbit map

\mathchar 28674\mathchar 12346\mathchar 8820_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29001}~\mathchar 28999\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29016

given by
\mathchar 28674\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28952\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28952\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
is an isomorphism of racks (see

). Furthermore, for any

, we have

\mathchar 28674\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29042}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28952\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28674\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28952\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29034\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28952\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 28956\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29034\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28952\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 28952\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 28674\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28952\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 28674\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28952\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 28674\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28952\mathchar 29000_{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
. Thus,

is an isomorphism of

-racks.
∎

6.
Modules over
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks

This section is devoted to identifying appropriate coefficient objects for constructing (co)homology theories for
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-racks. Introduced by Beck in
[
1
]
, these coefficient objects are commonly known as
Beck modules
in the literature. More precisely, given a category
\mathbf{\mathchar 28995}
and an object
\mathchar 29016
in
\mathbf{\mathchar 28995}
, a Beck module over
\mathchar 29016
is an abelian group object in the slice category
\mathbf{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29016
over
\mathchar 29016
. To exemplify their strength, we note that they simultaneously generalise the notion of coefficient modules from group cohomology, Lie algebra cohomology, as well as Hochschild cohomology of associative algebras. To avoid confusion, from now onwards, we shall write the maps
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}
and
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}
of a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}\delimiter 84054785
by
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29016}
and
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}
, respectively.

We begin by recalling some basic concepts from the theory of trunks, which was introduced by Fenn, Rourke, and Sanderson in
[
14
]
. A
trunk

\mathrm{\mathchar 29012}
is an object analogous to a category which consists of a class of objects and a set
\operatorname{\mathchar 29000\mathchar 29039\mathchar 29037}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29012}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28994\delimiter 84054785
of morphisms along with a number of commutative squares
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28993}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28994}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28995}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28996\mathchar 24891}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 29030}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 29031}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 29032}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 29033}

called
preferred squares
. Given two trunks
S
and
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012}
, a
trunk map

\mathchar 28998\mathchar 12346\textrm{S}\mathchar 12833\mathrm{\mathchar 29012}
is a map which assigns to each object
\mathchar 28993
of
S
, an object
\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785
of
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012}
, and to every morphism
\mathchar 29030\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28994
of
S
, a morphism
\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29030\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12833\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994\delimiter 84054785
of
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012}
such that the preferred square
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28993}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28994}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28995}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28996}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 29030}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 29031}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 29032}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 29033}

is mapped to the preferred square
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\delimiter 84054785}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28994\delimiter 84054785}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28995\delimiter 84054785}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28996\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29030\delimiter 84054785}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031\delimiter 84054785}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\delimiter 84054785}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchar 28998\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29033\delimiter 84054785}

For example, for any category
\mathbf{\mathchar 28995}
, we have a well-defined trunk
\mathrm{\mathchar 29012}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 84054785
, which has the same objects and morphisms as
\mathbf{\mathchar 28995}
, and whose preferred squares are the commutative diagrams in
\mathbf{\mathchar 28995}
. In particular, we denote the trunk associated to the category of abelian groups by
\mathbf{\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29026}
.

Given a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}\delimiter 84054785
, we define a trunk

over a fixed

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\tilde{\mathchar 8707}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\big\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28958_{\mathchar 29048\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29025\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 29048\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29026\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\,\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8707\mathchar 29049\big\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}\delimiter 84054785
be a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack and
\mathscr{\mathchar 28998}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28993\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28958\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28960\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28944\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28682\delimiter 84054785
an
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8707\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathchar 29016}\delimiter 84054785
-module. Then the semi-direct product
\delimiter 67273472{\mathscr{\mathchar 28998}\rtimes\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 24891\tilde{\mathchar 8707}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathscr{\mathchar 28998}\rtimes\mathchar 29016}\mathchar 24891\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathscr{\mathchar 28998}\rtimes\mathchar 29016}\delimiter 84054785
is a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack.

Proof.

Note that
{\mathscr{\mathchar 28998}\rtimes\mathchar 29016}
is a rack, as proved in
[
17
, Proposition 2.1]
. It remains to check that the maps
\mathtt{\mathchar 29045}_{\mathscr{\mathchar 28998}\rtimes\mathchar 29016}
and
\mathtt{\mathchar 29028}_{\mathscr{\mathchar 28998}\rtimes\mathchar 29016}
indeed give rise to a
\operatorname{\mathchar 28999\mathchar 29004}
-rack structure on
{\mathscr{\mathchar 28998}\rtimes\mathchar 29016}
.
